# IMPL-10_postprocessing_merge_quantize

# SPEC 10 — Postprocessing (Merge + Serving-Format Quantization)

> Aligned with the code of 2026-10-22.

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-02, IMPL-06.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` §13 steps 7–8, §13a (**vLLM-native serving formats**), §13b (quantization quality thresholds, absolute percentage points), §18 (artifact paths).

## Goal

Fold the trained adapter into the base model, and produce the quantized formats **the serving endpoint can actually load**. Quality thresholds are specified here and enforced before any quantized format is served.

## What changed from v1, and why

v1 produced `fp16` + `q5_k_m` GGUF files every cycle. GGUF is **llama.cpp's** format and the endpoint runs **vLLM**, so the pipeline spent conversion and eval compute on an artifact nothing could deploy — and there was no small format the server *could* open, leaving bf16 or nothing.

v1's own module docstring had the right of it: *"GGUF is the portable/edge path and vLLM on the merged model is the primary one."* It never followed through, and left `DEFAULT_FORMATS = ("fp16", "q5_k_m")` making a GGUF the per-cycle deliverable.

**GPU only** (decision 2026-10-19). The merge, every quantized export and the vLLM engine that serves and scores them run on CUDA and refuse to start without it (`common/gpu.py` `require_cuda`); nothing falls back to the CPU. Every deployment target is a vLLM format on a GPU. GGUF stays an **optional edge export** (§2a), never a deployment target.

## 1. `training/merge.py`

- Loads the base in **bf16** and folds in **one** adapter via PEFT `merge_and_unload()` → one standalone bf16 model.
- **One adapter, one merge.** v1 applied a Foundation LoRA and then a per-type LoRA on top, producing a model per document type — and the order mattered, because reversing it produced a different model from the one that was evaluated. That whole ordering problem is gone with the topology that created it (arch §4.1).
- A graduated per-type adapter (§4.2) is **never merged**. It is applied at serving time on top of these merged weights, one LoRA per request — the only shape vLLM can serve.
- Folds in the **checkpoint the §11.2 selector chose**, recorded on the plan (`MergePlan.selected_checkpoint`). `orchestration.pipeline_dag.stage_merge` reads it through `selected_checkpoint(ctx)`: this process's `checkpoint_eval` result, else the choice recorded in Blob at `eval-reports/v{n}/[scope/{scope}/]checkpoint_selection.json`. The merged weights do not say which checkpoint they came from, and once the staging volume is reclaimed nothing else does either.
- `dtype` defaults to **bf16, not fp16**: the adapter trained in bf16 against a bf16 base, and FP8 is quantized from this artifact. Merging to fp16 would insert a precision change between training and every serving format for no reason.
- Writes to the **staging volume**, per scope (`plan_merge(scope=)`): `staging/merged-models/{unified|scope/{scope}}/v{n}/`. The merged model is ~16 GB and quantization also runs on RunPod; pushing it to Azure and pulling it back is a 32 GB round trip for nothing (master §12a).
- **Runs on the GPU** into the **pinned local base** (see *Current implementation*); written to `<output>.partial` and renamed into place only once `config.json` and the safetensors exist.

## 2. `postprocessing/quantize.py` — serving formats

| Format | Tool | Hardware | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| **bf16 merged** | PEFT merge | Any CUDA GPU | Reference, and cycle 1's serving format |
| **FP8 (W8A8)** | llm-compressor | Native FP8 on Ada/Hopper (L4, L40S, H100, H200); weight-only on A100 | Default serving format from cycle 2 |
| **AWQ INT4 (W4A16)** | llm-compressor | Modern NVIDIA | VRAM-constrained serving only, if it meets threshold |

```bash
# fp8 is refused until it is verified (below); --formats bf16 is the default
python -m orchestration.run package --version v2 --release-id release-2026.11.1 --formats bf16 fp8
```

**bf16 is never re-exported.** It *is* the merged model; a copy would be a second 16 GB artifact identical to the first.

**With `--formats bf16` — the default (`defaults.formats` in `orchestration/config/pipeline.yaml`) — quantize is a no-op.** `_is_quantized` reports the stage complete as soon as merge is, and `quantize()` returns the merged model as the bf16 entry without exporting anything.

**FP8 is refused until verified.** `plan_quantization` defaults to bf16 alone and `quantize()` raises on `fp8` unless `fp8_verified=True` (`StageContext.fp8_verified`), which is to be set once Phase 0 spike item 9 has verified the export; no CLI flag sets it. An unverified serving format is a deployment that fails at load — or worse, one that loads and reads badly.

**The vision path is never quantized.** `NEVER_QUANTIZED = visual.*, mergers, aligners, lm_head`, passed to llm-compressor as its ignore list (arch §13a). Compressing it produces a model that loads cleanly and reads pages badly — very hard to notice, because it still emits well-formed JSON.

**Every serving format gets its own calibrator fit and its own gate run.** Quantization degrades exactly what was fine-tuned in, so an FP8 release inherits nothing from bf16's result. Today the evaluation gate scores bf16 only (its decision is `release_gate_decision(release, "bf16", tenant)`); a quantized format with no gate decision of its own leaves the release bundle at status `gated`, never `promoted`.

## 2a. GGUF — the edge export, on request only

`plan_gguf_export` / `export_gguf` produce a GGUF for an **offline or edge** deployment. Not per-cycle, not on the serving path, not a deployment target (the deployment is GPU only), and **validated separately in llama.cpp** against the §13b AWQ INT4 column — the serving gate does not cover it, because the serving endpoint never loads one. No command calls it, and past its guards `export_gguf` raises `NotImplementedError`: the llama.cpp conversion is not wired.

The v1 mmproj guard is carried over **unchanged**: Qwen3-VL is multimodal, so a GGUF needs the quantized LLM **plus a separate `mmproj`** file for the vision encoder and projector. Exporting without a verified one produces a model that loads and cannot see, which fails silently on every image-only document.

Serving and edge artifacts are stored under **different runtime prefixes** — `quantized-models/{scope}/v{n}/vllm/{format}/` and `.../gguf/{format}/`. They are loaded by different programs, and one prefix invites deploying the wrong one.

## 3. `postprocessing/quant_thresholds.py`

**Absolute percentage points against bf16, per field class** (arch §13b):

| Metric | FP8 max drop | AWQ INT4 max drop |
|---|---|---|
| Exact match — identifiers, money, dates | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| Match — names, addresses | 1.0 pp | 2.0 pp |
| List-field row recall | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| Confusable misattribution (increase) | 0.5 pp | 1.0 pp |
| ECE (increase) | 0.01 | 0.02 |
| Schema validity (constrained) | 100% | 100% |

`field_normalized_match` is judged on the **names, addresses** row and `field_exact_match` on the **identifiers, money, dates** row, each against its own allowance.

Three things about this table are deliberate:

- **Absolute, not relative.** v1 allowed a 2% *relative* field-F1 drop, which is 1.9 pp at F1 0.95 and 1.4 pp at 0.70 — so the rule got **stricter as the model got worse**, which is backwards. The same number should mean the same thing whatever the baseline.
- **Per field class.** A wrong policy number is a wrong extraction; a slightly-off entity name is usually still matchable. One allowance would let the forgiving class lend its slack to the unforgiving one.
- **Schema validity is a floor, not a margin.** Structured decoding guarantees it (§13), so anything below 100% means the guarantee is not working — an error, not a degraded result.

The reference is **bf16**. v1 measured every format against fp16, a GGUF format, so the reference itself was an artifact the endpoint could not load.

A format exceeding **any** threshold is not served. Asking the serving gate about a GGUF format raises and says where it *is* validated, rather than reading as "unsupported".

## 4. `postprocessing/validate_quant.py`

- Judges each produced format's metrics — from the extraction routine (IMPL-12) against the **frozen golden eval set** — against the table above. It scores nothing itself: the metrics are supplied (`StageContext.quant_metrics`), and with none supplied the quantize stage logs a warning instead of judging. No CLI path supplies them yet; a bf16-only cycle has nothing to compare, because bf16 *is* the reference.
- Also checks **row recall** and **confusable misattribution** on their own margins: a quantized model that keeps its field accuracy while dropping claim rows has lost exactly what a Loss Run is for, and an aggregate field score would not show it.
- Writes `quant_threshold_results` into the quantize stage result (`ValidationReport.manifest_entry()`) and **refuses promotion of an over-threshold format** (`assert_servable`) — no override flag.

## 5. Where the gates sit in `package`

`package` runs stages 8–11 in order (`orchestration.pipeline_dag.STAGES`): **quantize** (the §4 threshold gate at its end, when per-format metrics are supplied) → **calibrate** (IMPL-09) → **evaluation_gate** → **package** (the push).

- **The promotion gate** (IMPL-08) scores the merged bf16 model on the frozen golden eval set through serving, with the release's bf16 calibrators. It holds every floor; judges non-inferiority by a **paired bootstrap** over the per-document scores of the candidate's and the promoted release's eval reports (`paired_document_scores`, weighted as each metric counts its documents, and only on reports scored on the same frozen set of the tenant; otherwise a point comparison, with the reason in the decision's warnings); and blocks a release that improves no primary metric unless `--fixes-defect "<ticket>"` names the defect it fixes (on `package` and `all`, per scope when several run; recorded in the decision). A passing release is **production** at `field_normalized_match` ≥ 0.92 and **interim** below it — and **interim whatever its score** when its scope was scored on fewer than 150 frozen documents of a type it covers, with a warning in the decision. A failed gate is passed only by a written override (`--override-approver`, `--override-reason`, `--override-gate`: all three, one scope), recorded as a waiver.
- A blocked gate stops the release: the merged model stays on the staging volume, nothing is published, and the command exits non-zero with the per-metric verdicts. The push re-reads the recorded decision (`assert_gate_passed`) and refuses a version that did not pass, so a standalone `package` cannot publish it either. The release bundle carries the bf16 decision's tier.
- **Latency is recorded, not gated** (no latency SLA; decision 2026-10-19): `record_release_measurements` copies P95 latency per adapter and peak GPU memory from the eval report's `release_measurements` onto the bundle, and a missing measurement is a warning, never a block.

## Constraints

- Merged models never overwrite adapters (separate Blob paths).
- Every **served** quantized format passes threshold validation first — no exceptions, no override.
- The bf16 reference is always produced: every §13b threshold is a margin against it, so a run producing only a quantized format has nothing to measure its drop from.
- Record all produced formats in the manifest.
- Never emit a GGUF whose multimodal projector support is unverified.

## Acceptance checklist

- [ ] `merge` produces one loadable bf16 model from one adapter, folding in the selected checkpoint.
- [ ] `plan_quantization` defaults to **bf16 alone**, and refuses a GGUF format with an error naming the runtime mismatch.
- [ ] `quantize` refuses FP8 until `fp8_verified`, and never re-exports bf16.
- [ ] With `--formats bf16`, the quantize stage is complete once merge is and exports nothing.
- [ ] A serving plan without the bf16 reference is refused outright.
- [ ] `NEVER_QUANTIZED` covers the vision tower, every merger and `lm_head`.
- [ ] `export_gguf` refuses an unverified mmproj, and is reachable only on request.
- [ ] Serving and GGUF artifacts land under different runtime prefixes.
- [ ] `quant_thresholds` returns pass/fail correctly **at each format's exact boundary** — a format landing precisely on its allowance passes.
- [ ] Margins behave identically at F1 0.95 and 0.70, which is what "absolute" means.
- [ ] `validate_quant` refuses an over-threshold format **with no override path**, and refuses validation without the bf16 reference.
- [ ] A format that was not measured does not pass.
- [ ] **`assert_servable` refuses a serving format that produced no verdict at all.** `validate_quant` only returns a result for formats it was given metrics for, so a format nobody scored appears in neither `servable_formats` nor `blocked_formats`. Checking `blocked_formats` alone let it sail through to push with zero measurements — the precise inverse of the rule this module exists to enforce.
- [ ] The threshold gate sits inside `package`, between quantize and calibrate.
- [ ] `package` publishes an adapter as its `adapter_config.json` and `adapter_model.safetensors` only, never a checkpoint's resume state.
- [ ] The merge and every quantized export refuse to run without CUDA; nothing falls back to the CPU.
- [ ] The promotion gate runs inside `package`, after calibrate and before the push; the push refuses a version whose recorded gate decision did not pass.
- [ ] The release bundle's tier is the bf16 gate decision's: `interim` below 0.92, and `interim` whatever the score when its scope was scored on fewer than 150 frozen documents of a type.
- [ ] P95 latency and peak GPU memory are recorded on the release bundle and never block a release.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-21)

**Merge** (`training/merge.py`, run by `orchestration.pipeline_dag.stage_merge`), implemented:
- refuses an adapter directory without `adapter_config.json` (a run's output root is not an adapter), and
  a base revision still `PIN_ME` when the base would come from the Hub;
- loads the base with `AutoModelForImageTextToText` in the merge dtype on **`device_map="cuda"`** — GPU
  only (`require_cuda`); every engine is released before merge runs — from the pod's local copy
  (`model.local_dir`) when present, applies the adapter with PEFT, `merge_and_unload()`, saves safetensors
  (5 GB shards) **and the processor** (vLLM reads the chat template and image-processor config from the
  model directory), then renames `.partial` into place;
- merges the checkpoint `checkpoint_eval` selected (`selected_checkpoint`), read from Blob on a resumed run;
  with nothing selected a real run refuses with a `PipelineError` rather than merging the output root. The
  same reader decides which adapter `package` publishes, so the adapter published is the one merged;
- `finetune` merges in bf16; `all` takes `--dtype` (default `bf16`, `defaults.dtype`).

**Quantization plan is scope-aware** (`plan_quantization(..., scope=)`): a scoped run quantizes its own
merged model into its own paths. With `--formats bf16` the stage does nothing. The llm-compressor export
(`_export`) refuses without CUDA and is not wired yet — it raises `NotImplementedError` naming the recipe and
the ignore list — and needs its **own environment** (`requirements-quantize.txt`; PyPI name `llmcompressor`):
it requires `datasets>=4` where ms-swift 3 needs `<4`, and a transformers range vLLM 0.11 excludes.

**Package uploads real weights** (`orchestration.pipeline_dag._push_weights`, through
`artifact_registry/transfer.py`), after the release bundle and its routing check are built (IMPL-13 §4):
- the selected adapter, by `push_scoped_adapter`, to `adapters/foundation/v{n}/` (unified) or
  `adapters/scope/{scope}/v{n}/` — **only** `adapter_config.json` and `adapter_model.safetensors`
  (`ADAPTER_FILES`), never the checkpoint's optimizer and scheduler state (~1.5 GB of a rank-64 checkpoint).
  `finetune --push-adapters` publishes the same two files;
- the merged model to `merged-models/{unified|scope/{scope}}/v{n}/`;
- every non-bf16 format to `quantized-models/{unified|scope/{scope}}/v{n}/vllm/{format}/`.

The adapter and merged-model pushes skip every file whose bytes Blob already holds, compared by the MD5
recorded with each upload (`BlobClient.upload_dir(..., skip_identical=True)`), so re-packaging a release
sends nothing; a blob that records no MD5 is sent again. A dry run still records where each would go. The run
manifest's `quantized_model` points at the first quantized format — never bf16, which is not an export.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line and dated *Current implementation* 2026-10-13.
- §1: the merge reads the selection through `selected_checkpoint` (process result, else `checkpoint_selection.json` in Blob); the staging output path is per scope.
- §2: `--formats bf16` (the default) makes quantize a no-op; `fp8_verified` has no CLI setter; the FP8 example notes it is refused until verified.
- §4: `validate_quant` judges supplied metrics only; no CLI supplies them yet, so the stage warns.
- *Current implementation*: the published adapter is `adapter_config.json` + `adapter_model.safetensors` only (also for `--push-adapters`); adapter and merged-model pushes skip identical files by MD5; the bundle and routing check come before any push; the llm-compressor export is not wired.
- Acceptance checklist: added the bf16 no-op and the two-file adapter publish.

## Changes since 2026-10-13

- Header: aligned with the code of 2026-10-21; architecture reference is `finetuning-architecture-v2.4.docx`.
- What changed from v1: GPU only (decision 2026-10-19); GGUF an optional edge export, never a deployment target.
- §2: the bf16 reference runs on any CUDA GPU, not any hardware.
- §2a: GGUF is not a deployment target; no command calls `export_gguf`, and the llama.cpp conversion is not wired.
- §5 (new): the stage order in `package`; the promotion gate's paired bootstrap, `--fixes-defect` and the interim cap for a small eval set; the push re-checks the decision; the bundle's tier; latency recorded, not gated (no SLA).
- Acceptance checklist: GPU only; the gate's place and the push's check; the bundle's tier and the small-set cap; latency never blocks.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (code review)

- §5: the gate's bootstrap is weighted and pairs only reports scored on the same frozen set of the tenant; the interim cap follows what the scope was scored on; `--fixes-defect` per scope; the written override on the command line (IMPL-08).
- Acceptance checklist: the bundle's tier under the per-scope cap.
- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` and the code of 2026-10-22.

# IMPL-11_serving

# SPEC 11 — Serving (vLLM Endpoint + Classifier + Routing)

> Aligned with the code of 2026-10-22.

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-07 (inference core), IMPL-09 (calibration), IMPL-10 (postprocessing).
>
> **Naming hazard (master §0.1):** the architecture's "**Fideon SPEC_11**" is the external Presidio de-identification spec (implemented in our IMPL-05). This file is our serving layer. Different documents.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` §0a (audit gate contract), §0b (LoB output), §0e (decisions of 2026-10-19: every page read), §4a (**classifier as a load-bearing component**), §4b (ACORD two-level), §4c (day-zero classifier), §5 (confidence), §7 (long documents: every page, read in windows), §14 (**RunPod split: what runs where**).

## Goal

Serve the promoted model on a RunPod Serverless vLLM endpoint with per-release adapter routing, document-type classification, windowed reading of every page of a long document, and calibrated confidence post-processing — built on the shared inference core so **serving == eval == testing**.

## The infrastructure boundary (arch §14) — get this right or the design leaks

```
Training (ephemeral pod)              Serving (persistent endpoint)
─────────────────────────             ─────────────────────────────
clone repo @ commit                   vLLM + promoted model
pull base + corpus (Blob)                     │
run LoRA training                     request ──> classify doc type
push adapter + manifest (Blob)                │
terminate                             select + hot-swap adapter
                                              │
External orchestrator                 inference + logprobs
triggers pods via RunPod API                  │
                                       calibrate confidence
Application logic (CPU infra,                 │
outside RunPod): OCR, prompt          return JSON + confidence
assembly, endpoint calls,
confidence calibration
```

**RunPod is scoped purely to GPU-bound work.** Application/orchestration logic — calling MinerU, assembling the prompt, calling the endpoint, applying the calibration transform, returning final JSON — runs in **your own service** (Azure Function, App Service, or AKS), not inside RunPod. This keeps business logic on cheap CPU infra, keeps it portable if the GPU provider changes, and keeps the RunPod handler small. Document this boundary in the module docstring; it is an architectural constraint, not a deployment preference.

## Deliverables

### 1. `serving/vllm_entrypoint.py`
- RunPod Serverless handler wrapping vLLM, serving the promoted merged/quantized model over an OpenAI-compatible API, **with logprobs enabled** (calibration depends on them).
- **One LoRA per request, never a reload.** With one promoted release covering every line, the engine is that release's merged model and no adapter is applied. With several releases — or one that leaves lines to the base model — the engine loads the **base with LoRA enabled** and each release is its adapter, applied per request (`serves_base_with_loras`, `load_release_runtimes`). A §4.2 graduated per-type adapter would be applied the same way, from the classifier result. One LoRA per request is what vLLM supports, which is exactly why the v1 Foundation-plus-per-type stack could never have been served.
- Pulls the promoted releases' adapters and calibrators from Blob on cold start (IMPL-02); version configurable. The model itself is handed to `cold_start` — loading it there is not wired yet, and `cold_start` refuses without one.
- Uses IMPL-07 `model_runner` / `input_builder` for generation so output matches eval and testing exactly.
- **Asserts the serving MinerU version matches the training corpus pin** (IMPL-03 `assert_version_matches`) wherever MinerU is installed — a mismatch is distribution shift and a regression trigger (arch §8a), not a warning to ignore. A serving pod without MinerU does no OCR; it logs the pin its requests' page texts must come from.
- **Every tuneable in `configs/inference/vllm_serving.yaml` must have a reader.** `serving_thresholds()` lifts `routing.classifier_confidence_threshold`, `confidence.review_threshold`, `routing.detect_lob`, `routing.lob_confidence_threshold`, `routing.lob_family_threshold` and `routing.fallback_doc_type` out of the config and passes them into `extract`; `release_pins()` reads `routing.release_pins`. (There is no page threshold to read: every page is read, in windows planned by the function the corpus build planned its training rows with, so nothing in serving's config can re-shape documents away from what was trained. The `long_documents` block keeps only `conflict_resolution: declarations_page_wins`, which names the rule the window merge applies by group order; no code reads it.) They were declared in that file and read by no code, so an operator could raise `review_threshold`, redeploy, and change nothing at all — the hardcoded defaults inside `extract` won silently every time. A test asserts each key the helper emits is a parameter `extract` accepts.
- **Calibration is selected after classification, not before.** The handler hands the whole `{doc_type: params}` map to `extract`, which picks the entry once the classifier has said what the document is. Resolving it in the handler from the caller-supplied `doc_type` meant a lookup on `""` for every request that did not name its own type — which is every classification-driven request, the endpoint's entire purpose — and the request was refused before extraction ran. `assert_calibration_present` accepts the map and checks it is non-empty; an empty one is still no calibration. It is checked only when no release is served, or some served release has no calibrators of its own.
- **The adapter map holds adapters that exist.** `resolve_model_version(..., doc_type=…)` constructs `type_adapter` unconditionally — it is a path, not evidence anything was trained — so `build_adapter_map` confirms the directory holds an `adapter_config.json` before mapping a doc type to it. Trusting the path mapped all three types on a `--foundation-only` build, and vLLM was then handed a `LoRARequest` for a directory that does not exist, failing every request instead of serving Foundation-only with a `routing:no_adapter_available` flag.
- **No plaintext PII in logs**; do not persist raw request/response bodies (master §8). The payload log call sits **inside** the handler's `try`: `safe_log_payload` iterates the payload, so an `input` that is not a JSON object raised straight out of the handler and RunPod returned an opaque platform error — the exact failure the structured-error contract exists to prevent.

### 2. `serving/doc_type_classifier.py`
**This is load-bearing, not preprocessing (arch §4a).** If classification is wrong, you load the wrong adapter *and* the wrong prompt *and* the wrong schema, and the extraction fails no matter how good the model is.

```
document ──> classifier ──> confidence >= threshold ? ──> yes ──> select adapter + prompt + schema
                                     │                              (acord -> + form number)
                                     └── no ──> Foundation-only extraction + human routing review
```

- Identifies doc type ∈ {`acord`, `policy`, `lossrun`} **and the ACORD form number** — the two-level classification of arch §4b. Returns label + confidence.
- Reads a document's **first two pages** — their images by page number and their text joined, cut at 4,000 characters (`classifier_input`, `classifier_messages`) — the same rule a training row is built by.
- **Default implementation: zero-shot via the Foundation model** (arch §4a Option C) using `doc_type_classifier_prompt.jinja` — zero extra infrastructure, and the Foundation already understands these document types.
- **Also reads a policy's line of business** when asked (`Classification.lob`), with the probability of the line's own tokens as its confidence (`line_confidence`) — never a number the model writes about itself. Used only with `routing.detect_lob` on, which ships **off** (§6, *Current implementation*). With detection on and no classifier supplied, the endpoint builds `ZeroShotClassifier` on the engine's base weights with no adapter (`line_classifier`) — only when the engine holds the base; an engine holding one release's merged model reads no line. The line decides which release's adapter reads the policy, so no adapter can have been chosen yet.
- Interface is swappable for a **dedicated vision classifier** (Option B) later, which is the right escalation if classification accuracy becomes a *measured* bottleneck — it works in both OCR and image-only modes, unlike a text-feature classifier.
- **Day-zero (arch §4c):** before Foundation v1.0 exists, the classifier runs against the **base** `Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct`. This path is explicitly temporary and is superseded on promotion of Foundation v1.0, after which the base model is retired from the production inference path.
- **Has its own accuracy target and is measured on the golden eval set** alongside extraction metrics (IMPL-08 `classifier_accuracy`) — a classifier at 92% caps the whole system at 92%.
- A request that names its `doc_type` (and `acord_form`) skips classification (`StaticClassifier`); with no classifier held and detection off, a request without `doc_type` has nothing to classify it.

### 3. `serving/adapter_router.py`
- Maps classifier result → (adapter path, prompt file, schema). Deterministic selection.
- **Low-confidence fallback (arch §4a):** when the classifier isn't confident, **don't silently guess**. Fall back to **Foundation-only extraction** with a generic schema-agnostic prompt and flag the document for human routing review. A wrong-adapter extraction is worse than a slightly-generic one.
- Runs **before** adapter selection in both the serving path and the testing routine.
- For ACORD, resolves the form number to the matching schema even when a single shared `acord` adapter is in use (arch §4b Option 1) — the classifier must identify the specific form so the correct schema is always selected.

### 4. `serving/page_router.py`
Long-document handling, primarily a **Policy-document** concern (arch §7). Policy documents can run 50+ pages; each page at full resolution costs 1,000–2,000+ vision tokens, so a whole policy cannot go into one prompt. **Every page is read, chunk by chunk, never selected pages** (decision 2026-10-19, arch §0e). Page selection was removed: a page that scored nothing was never read, at training or at serving, and whatever it printed was silently missing.

1. **Declarations page** — `plan_pages` returns **every page**, in order, and the first page whose MinerU text matches a declarations signal (`declarations_page`). The other `PAGE_SIGNALS` (coverage schedule and coverage summary 6; schedule 5; named insured, policy number, policy period 4; premium, deductible, limits of insurance 3; endorsement, producer 2) stay on the plan's scores for tracing and decide nothing: detecting a page labels it and drops nothing. There is no page threshold, no selection threshold and no `RoutingPlan.routed`. `data_pipeline.dataset_builder.policy_windows.pages_to_read` wraps it — `(every page, declarations page)` — so training and serving plan by one rule; with no OCR text (`image_only`) every page is read and no declarations page is detected.
2. **Windowed extraction (policies)** — every policy (every policy's output is canonical) is read as **section group × page window**, planned by `policy_windows.plan_windows` over every page, the same function the corpus build expanded its training rows with. **Every group reads every page** (`common.schema_sections.pages_for`; the page rules in `configs/schema_sections.yaml` are `every` and `declarations`). `decl` (and `dtd` where a line carries it) takes as its first window pages 1–5 (`pages.declarations_leading: 5`) plus the detected declarations page, read together (`leading_pages`); the pages after them follow in windows, as every other group's do. Windows hold `pages_per_extraction_call` pages, with split runs overlapping by `run_overlap_pages` (1 page on the common model), and a window whose pages' own OCR text would not fit its call is split into windows that do (`expand_tasks.fit_windows_to_text`, the rule the corpus build sizes its rows with; `image_only` windows by page count alone). Each window's prompt and decoding schema are its group's slice. More windows per policy than under page selection, so a longer read. See §6.
3. **Other types** — there is no routed single call. A Loss Run is windowed only when its estimated rows overflow one answer; anything else (an ACORD form, a Loss Run that fits) is read in one call over every page, interleaved `[img_1][<page 1 of N> + md_1][img_2][<page 2 of N> + md_2]…` (IMPL-07). One call per page was the earlier design and was wrong twice over: it asked the model to produce a complete document-level JSON from one page — a shape no training row ever had — and it prevented the model from seeing that a table on page 9 continues on page 10. Interleaving makes a policy window a **subsequence of the full document**, so the served shape is the trained shape with fewer pairs.
4. **Merge** — a policy's windows are merged by `serving/policy_merge.merge_policy_windows`, a long Loss Run's by `serving/lossrun_merge.merge_extracted_windows` (§6). `merge_page_extractions` (the declarations-page-wins rule for per-page results) stays in `page_router.py` as code only; it is not on the serving path, and the declarations rule is stated to the model in the Policy prompt and applied by the window merge's group order.

The page numbers in the markers are the document's real numbers, so a window's numbering tells the model it is holding a fragment: fields living on pages it was not shown are absent, not missing. `image_only` carries the markers without markdown — the guarantee is *no OCR text*, not *no text at all*.

- **No document is page-routed** — a policy of any length is read over every page, in windows; there is no per-scope list of routed types (`long_doc_types` is gone).
- **Record which pages fed each extraction** in the output metadata (`pages_used`, master §9), so a low-confidence field can be traced to the specific page it came from. A policy's `pages_used` is every page.

### 5. Confidence post-processing — composed in `serving/pipeline.py`
*(`calibration/apply_calibration.py` + `calibration/list_completeness.py`, called from the pipeline, rather than a `serving/confidence_postprocess.py` wrapper. The steps below are all implemented; a serving-side wrapper around two calibration modules would be a second place for the output contract to be shaped, and the contract has to have exactly one.)*
- Applies IMPL-09 calibration to raw logprob confidence (the release's per-field-type calibrators and thresholds when it has them; otherwise the v1 transform, with every field flagged `:uncalibrated`), adds the **list-completeness** signal, shapes the final output contract (master §9), and flags sub-threshold fields for review.
- Emits `line_of_business`: for a policy, the line resolution `{lines, source, confidence, candidates, hypothesis_agreed}` (`source` ∈ `caller`, `detected`, `detected_uncertain`, `undetected`); otherwise the standard `{value, confidence}` shape (arch §0b) collapsed from the line spans, `null` when undetermined.
- **Validates the final JSON against the doc_type schema before returning** — this mirrors the Fideon SPEC_07 Stage 3 audit gate (arch §0a). A schema-invalid response is an error, not a returned result. On a common-model line the served JSON (every key filled) is what is judged, and a required section no window wrote, or an object written empty (`minProperties`), still fails. The judgement is one function, `serving.pipeline.schema_verdict` (a `SchemaVerdict`: the answer as judged, the filled answer, `valid`, `errors`), shared with evaluation: `served_schema_verdict` envelopes a merged common-model answer and fills its system fields first, then judges it the same way, so checkpoint selection (`evaluation/metrics/coverage_metrics.score_schema_validity`) judges a whole common-model document as serving would return it. Checked as written, the merged answer lacked what the pipeline adds and `document_schema_validity_rate` read a false 0%.
- **Fills every key of the line's schema** (`common.canonical.with_all_keys`, without the client's `fideon:` annotation properties) after validation and confidence: a value not extracted is `null`, not absent, so every served policy JSON — base model or trained — has the same keys. A post-process, never trained.
- Never returns raw (uncalibrated) confidence as if calibrated — IMPL-09 raises when calibration is missing.

### 6. `serving/pipeline.py` (request orchestrator)
Per request: classify (or take the caller's `doc_type`) → route adapter/prompt/schema → resolve the policy's line (caller's; detected only with `detect_lob` on) → route through the serving plan (release, layout family, or base model + `_fallback.json`) → read: policy windows / Loss Run windows / one call → merge → serving guards (common-model lines) → IMPL-09 confidence → list completeness → canonical envelope → schema validation → every-key fill → final JSON.

**This is the canonical pipeline the testing harness (IMPL-12) reuses**, which is what guarantees test == prod. Testing must call it, never re-implement it. The golden eval (IMPL-08) reads through it too, and checkpoint selection's whole-document pass merges its windows with the same `serving/policy_merge.py`.

## Constraints
- Non-GPU orchestration runs **outside** RunPod; the RunPod handler stays scoped to inference (arch §14). Document the boundary.
- Deterministic adapter/prompt/schema selection.
- Calibrated (never raw) confidence in responses.
- Every response schema-validated before return.
- MinerU version parity with the training corpus asserted.
- No PII in logs; no persisted raw request/response bodies.
- Line-of-business detection stays off (`routing.detect_lob: false`) until it has been measured.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] Endpoint serves a request end-to-end → schema-shaped JSON + calibrated confidence + `line_of_business`.
- [ ] Where a type has graduated, the correct per-type adapter is hot-swapped from the classification result; otherwise the merged model serves it with no adapter, and that is NOT flagged for review — flagging the expected path would queue every document.
- [ ] ACORD classification returns both `doc_type` and `acord_form`, and the form selects the schema.
- [ ] Low classifier confidence → **Foundation-only fallback + review flag**, never a silent guess.
- [ ] A policy of any length is read in windows over **every page**, and its `pages_used` is every page; a page no section signal matches is read like any other.
- [ ] The declarations group's (and `dtd`'s) first window is pages 1–5 plus the detected declarations page, read together; a declarations page behind a cover sheet joins it; every later page follows in windows.
- [ ] A window's user turn is a **subsequence** of the full document's — the same blocks, fewer pairs. Comparing system prompts alone does not catch a divergence here.
- [ ] `pages_used` reflects the pages actually sent, including for `image_only` documents that carry no per-page OCR text.
- [ ] Nothing in serving's config or a release's scope selects pages: there is no page threshold and no `long_doc_types`.
- [ ] A window that fails (unparseable, or cut at the token limit) is split in half and retried; one that still fails alone is dropped and flagged `window_failed:<group>:p<page>`, and the rest of the document is returned.
- [ ] A served policy JSON carries every key of its line's schema, `null` where nothing was extracted.
- [ ] A policy with no line, detection off, is refused unless the caller sends `allow_lob_fallback`; a known line no promoted release covers is read by the base model against `_fallback.json`, flagged `route:lob_fallback_used`.
- [ ] A schema-invalid generation is rejected, not returned.
- [ ] Checkpoint selection judges a merged common-model answer with serving's verdict (`served_schema_verdict`), not a stricter copy of it.
- [ ] On a common-model line, a form's edition left in its form number in an unambiguous shape is moved to the form's empty `edition_date` (`MM/YY`) and takes the number's span; a filled `edition_date` is never overwritten.
- [ ] Missing calibration for the served version raises rather than returning raw confidence.
- [ ] MinerU version mismatch against the corpus pin fails loudly.
- [ ] No PII in logs; no raw bodies persisted.
- [ ] `serving/pipeline.py` is the single path reused by IMPL-12.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-21)

**Canonical policies are read as windows over every page** (`serving/pipeline.py::_extract_policy_windows`):
every section group × page window planned by `data_pipeline/dataset_builder/policy_windows.plan_windows`
over every page (`pages_to_read`: every page, and the declarations page `serving.page_router.plan_pages`
finds in the same text the training rows were planned on), whatever the document's length. The routed
single-call branch, the page threshold and `long_doc_types` are gone from `extract`. Each round's windows
go to vLLM in **batches of at most 32** (`MAX_WINDOWS_PER_BATCH`, chosen, not measured; `generate_batch`), so a
200-page policy's page images are not all decoded at once. vLLM fails every prompt of a batch when it refuses one, so a batch whose windows all failed with one error is read again window by window, and only the window that caused it fails. Each window's answer limit is its section task's reserved budget
(`common.config.answer_cap(task_for(group))`), its page references are bounded to its own pages
(`common.schemas.with_page_bounds`), and an answer cut at the token limit is refused even if it parses. A
window that fails is **split in half and retried** in the next round (decoding is greedy, so an unchanged
retry would write the same thing); one that still fails at a single page is dropped and flagged
`window_failed:<group>:p<page>`. Only when no window succeeds is the request an error. `window_latencies_ms`
holds one entry per batch (and per window read alone after a whole-batch failure). This is how **200+ page
policies** are served. At cold start `assert_window_plan_served` refuses a release serving policies whose
`window_plan_version` is not this code's `window_plan_id()` — one that records none was packaged before every
page was read.

**The window merge** (`serving/policy_merge.py::merge_policy_windows`):
- *Self-contained lines* (the frozen non-SPEC_21 schemas): windows merged in group order then page order, so
  `decl` wins a conflict; a later differing value is kept out and flagged `<path>:merge_conflict`; arrays
  concatenate and are de-duplicated on the `configs/schema_sections.yaml` `array_keys`, a duplicate keeping
  the more complete row and the union of page references.
- *Common-model lines* (the seven personal lines, `gl`, and the fallback — no line): each window's ids are
  namespaced; **units** (locations, buildings, vehicles, drivers, watercraft, scheduled items, LOB parts) are
  joined on their own keys (`unit_keys`: VIN, location number, ...) and every reference rewritten to the
  merged unit; a row read twice because two windows share an overlap page is dropped from one of them — never
  a unit's row (`_drop_shared_page_rows`); the other tables are joined on the line's keys with references
  compared through the units they name, and a row whose reference its window could not see joins the one row
  it matches (`_join_unreferenced`); the whole document is then renumbered in printed order
  (`common.structural_ids.renumber_structural_ids`, the numbering training uses). Flags:
  `:joined_without_identifier`, `:ambiguous_unit`, `:unplaced_fragment`, `:dangling_reference`.

**Serving guards on common-model lines** (`serving/conventions.py`, after the merge):
- `conform` applies the label rules (`data_pipeline.ingestion.label_rules.apply_label_rules`) against every
  page's text: each value once, citing every page that prints it; an extra field moved into its own empty
  field. A value no page prints is flagged `<path>:not_printed`; on a born-digital document
  (`ocr_meta.modality` native) an extra field no page prints is dropped, flagged
  `additional_fields:not_printed_dropped`. A scan's OCR text settles nothing, so on a scan every value stays.
- `conform` also moves a form's edition left inside its form number to the form's empty `edition_date`, as the
  labels now do (`label_rules.split_form_edition`): only an unambiguous shape — ISO's `AA 99 99 MM YY`, or a
  month and year in brackets after the number — parsed `MM/YY`, the number kept without it; a filled
  `edition_date` is never overwritten. The moved edition takes the number's span, since it was read in the
  number's tokens.
- `likely_missed` flags `<block>.<field>:likely_missed` where a declarations field was left empty while a line
  of any page opens with its label and a value of its type (`_likely_missed` passes every page: the
  declarations windows read every page).
- `unit_link_flags` flags `coverages[i].applies_to:unit_link_unknown` for a coverage served without its unit
  when the answer holds two or more vehicles, boats or buildings, or another row of its code does carry a link.
- Empty optional objects and empty rows are dropped from the answer (`_without_empty`).

**Long Loss Runs** (`lossrun_windows`, `_extract_lossrun_windows`): windowed only when the estimated rows of
the whole document overflow one answer (`answer_cap("lossrun_rows")`, 8,192 tokens), with the same split-and-
retry rule; merged by `serving/lossrun_merge.merge_extracted_windows` and reconciled against the printed totals.
Flags `claims:merge_conflict`, `claims:totals_mismatch`; the output carries `reconciliation`.

**The request** (`serving/vllm_entrypoint.py::build_request`):
- `lob` (or `line_of_business`): one line, or a list for a package policy, normalised to lower case with classic
  auto read as `personal_auto` (`common.lob.merge_line`). It selects the line's canonical schema; a single line
  with no schema is **refused** unless the caller sends `allow_lob_fallback`.
- `lob_hypothesis`: a line L1/L2 suspects without being sure; reconciled with the line the classifier reads.
- `source_file_name` (or `file_name`): written into the canonical output by the system, never asked of the model.
- `page_texts` — MinerU's per-page markdown, from MinerU 3.4.5's pipeline backend at the corpus pin — must number
  exactly pages `1..n` against the `n` images sent, or the request is refused; a joined `ocr_text` is accepted for
  a single page only. Images are paired **by page number** (`page_N.` by file name, else position); a page beyond
  the images is an error. `ocr_meta` carries the document's modality.

**Line of business** (`serving/pipeline.py::_resolve_lob`): the caller's line wins (flagged
`lob:caller_disagrees` when a confident classifier reads another). **Detection ships off**
(`routing.detect_lob: false`) until `scripts/measure_lob_detection.py` has measured it on held-out policies; off,
a policy with no line is refused, or read against the fallback when the caller sends `allow_lob_fallback`. On: a
line read at or above `lob_confidence_threshold` (0.90) routes as the caller's would; at or above
`lob_family_threshold` (0.60) it routes to that line's family, flagged `lob:uncertain_within_family`; below, the
base model reads the policy against the fallback, flagged `lob:undetected`.

**Routing by layout family** (`serving/release_router.py::ServingPlan.route`, `configs/layout_families.yaml`:
`personal_lines` = homeowners, personal_auto, dwelling_fire, ocean_marine, motorcycle, recreational_vehicle,
personal_umbrella; `casualty_fleet` = gl, auto, wc):
- a rollback pin (`routing.release_pins`) takes every document of its type;
- a policy whose line is missing or in no family goes to an unrestricted release (unified, policy) still serving
  policies; with none, to the base-model fallback when the caller allowed it (or detection found no line);
  otherwise it is refused;
- a routable line goes to the narrowest promoted release whose `lines` cover all of its lines (newest on a tie;
  a package of common-model lines never reaches a line-scoped release);
- a known line no promoted release covers is read by the **base model with no LoRA against `_fallback.json`**,
  flagged `route:lob_fallback_used`, with the release's calibrators dropped (every field goes to review). It is
  refused when the engine holds a release's merged model rather than the base (`base_model_loaded`).
`route` in the output records `layout_family`, `lob_fallback_used` and `lob_source`. The release step records
the same routing as a table on the bundle and fails when a line cannot be routed (`serving/routing_check.py`,
IMPL-13 §4).

**Serving through the chosen release** (`serving/vllm_entrypoint.load_release_runtimes`, `extract(...,
release_runtimes=)`): cold start builds, per served release, its adapter and its fitted calibrators and
thresholds (`releases/.../calibration/{format}/calibrators.json`). With **one** release covering every line the
engine serves its merged model and no adapter is applied; with **several**, or one that leaves a known line to
the base model (`serves_base_with_loras`), the engine loads the base with LoRA enabled and each release's adapter
is downloaded to `staging/serving-adapters/<release_id>/` and applied per request — cold start refuses a model
loaded without LoRA support (`enable_lora: false`), or a release whose adapter is missing. `extract` routes each
request through the release `route` returned. The v1 per-version calibration is needed only for a release
without its own calibrators. An operator **pin** outranks line-scoped routing, so a rollback applies to every
document of the type. Cold start does not require a unified run when releases are promoted. No scope lists
page-routed types any more: `long_doc_types` and `long_doc_types_for` were removed, and every served type
reads every page.

**Parity with training**: the same blank-page placeholder, the same pixel budget (engine
`mm_processor_kwargs`), the same window plan over every page (`pages_to_read`, `plan_windows`), dates in `MM/DD/YYYY` by the same word rule,
the same classifier input, and the calibration page text by the same definition
(`calibration.features.shown_ocr_text`).

**Cold start** (`assert_prompt_hash`): each release records the hash of **every prompt input** — all
templates (`prompts/**/*.jinja`, the per-doc-type ones included), every schema (ours and the client's
canonical files) and the section map. A release packaged with different prompt inputs is **refused**; one
with no recorded hash is warned about.

**Engine**: vLLM `==0.11.0` (the build calibration was fitted with); refuses to start without CUDA; loads the
merged model, or the base from the pod's local copy.

**OCR pin at cold start**: the corpus manifest must still record `mineru_version`, but the version comparison
runs only where MinerU is installed. A serving pod does no OCR (requests carry page texts produced by the pinned
MinerU), and comparing against a MinerU it does not have refused every deployment. A real mismatch is a
`ColdStartError`.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line and dated *Current implementation* 2026-10-13.
- §1: base + LoRA per request when several releases are promoted or a release leaves lines to the base model; `serving_thresholds()` also reads `detect_lob`, the two line thresholds and `fallback_doc_type`; `release_pins()`; the model is supplied to `cold_start`.
- §2: the classifier's two-page input; line-of-business reading with token-probability confidence, used only with detection on (ships off); the base-model line classifier.
- §4: replaced "one call for the selected pages, no merge" for policies with routed-page windows (`plan_windows`: decl over 5 leading pages + declarations page, arrays/lineblk over routed pages, 1-page overlap); documented the page signals, the ≤5-page rule and that short documents are still scored; the single interleaved call is now the path for other long types; merges are `policy_merge` / `lossrun_merge`.
- §5/§6: line-resolution output shape; validation of the filled answer on common-model lines; every-key post-process; updated request flow.
- *Current implementation*: window batching, answer caps, page bounds, split-and-retry, `window_failed`; common-model merge (units, joins, overlap duplicates, renumbering) and its flags; serving guards (`not_printed`, extra-field drop on born-digital, `likely_missed`, `unit_link_unknown`, empty-object drop); Loss Run windows; new request keys (`lob_hypothesis`, `allow_lob_fallback`, `source_file_name`, classic auto → personal auto, MinerU 3.4.5 page texts); line resolution with detection off; routing by layout family with the base-model fallback (replacing "refused"); the release routing table.
- Acceptance checklist: replaced the single-call policy item; added window retry, every-key output and fallback routing items.

## Changes since 2026-10-13

- Header and goal: aligned with the code of 2026-10-21; architecture reference `finetuning-architecture-v2.4.docx` (§0e added); "page routing" replaced by windowed reading of every page.
- §1: no page threshold to read (`DEFAULT_LONG_DOC_PAGE_THRESHOLD` is gone); `long_documents` keeps only `conflict_resolution`, read by no code.
- §4: rewritten for every page read (decision 2026-10-19): `plan_pages` returns every page and only detects the declarations page; `pages_to_read` replaces `routed_pages`; every group reads every page, the declarations group's (and `dtd`'s) first window pages 1–5 plus the declarations page; the routed single-call branch, `SELECTION_THRESHOLD`, `RoutingPlan.routed`, the page cap and `long_doc_types` removed.
- §5: the schema judgement is `schema_verdict`, shared with evaluation through `served_schema_verdict`.
- Acceptance checklist: every-page and declarations-window items replace the routed-page and page-cap items; new items for the served schema verdict and form editions.
- *Current implementation*: windows over every page; `conform` moves a form's edition to `edition_date` with the number's span; `likely_missed` reads every page; `long_doc_types_for` removed; parity names the window plan, not a page threshold.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (code review)

- Windows split by their pages' own OCR text, by the rule the corpus build uses (`fit_windows_to_text`); `image_only` windows by page count alone.
- A round's windows go to vLLM in batches of at most 32 (`MAX_WINDOWS_PER_BATCH`, chosen, not measured), one latency entry per batch.
- Header: aligned with `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` and the code of 2026-10-22.
- A batch whose windows all failed with one error is read again window by window; latency is one entry per batch.
- Cold start refuses a policy release whose recorded window plan is not this code's (`assert_window_plan_served`).

# IMPL-12_testing_extraction_routine

# SPEC 12 — Testing / Extraction Routine (CLI Harness)

> Aligned with the code of 2026-10-22.

> Read `IMPL-00_MASTER_CONTEXT.md` first. Dependencies: IMPL-01, IMPL-02, IMPL-03 (OCR), IMPL-07 (inference core), IMPL-08 (metrics), IMPL-09 (calibration), IMPL-11 (serving pipeline).
>
> **Naming hazard (master §0.1):** the architecture's "**Fideon SPEC_12**" is the external multi-tenant deployment spec. This file is our testing harness. Different documents.
>
> **Architecture refs:** `finetuning-architecture-v2.5.docx` §17 (**extraction/testing routine**), §19 (folder placement), §13a (quantized-format validation consumer), §5 (confidence without ground truth).

## Goal

A CLI harness to test any fine-tuned model version — or the base model with a training checkpoint applied — on real documents post-training, producing structured JSON + per-field/overall confidence + quality metrics, with version-organized outputs. The documents are ones the pipeline has already imported and OCR'd, read exactly as serving reads one (page images + MinerU page text). **It mirrors the exact production inference path (MinerU text → model → JSON) by reusing the serving pipeline (IMPL-11), so what you test is what you ship.**

## Deliverables

### 1. Folder layout under `testing/`
```
testing/
├── run_extraction.py            # CLI entrypoint (also reached as `orchestration.run extract`)
├── compare_models.py            # base vs base + adapter vs gold, per document (§6)
├── comparison.py                # field-by-field comparison, the all-fields report, Excel workbooks
├── render_prompts.py            # writes prompts/ from common.prompts
├── prompts/                     # reference renders of the prompts serving sends (generated)
│   ├── acord_25.prompt.txt, acord_125.prompt.txt, acord_140.prompt.txt
│   ├── lossrun.prompt.txt
│   ├── policy.prompt.txt, policy_<line>.prompt.txt               # + *.image_only.prompt.txt
│   └── policy_<line>.{decl,arrays,lineblk}.prompt.txt            # window prompts, common-model lines
├── ocr_cache/                   # page images fetched from processed/ in Blob, kept between runs
├── results/{version}/           # OUTPUT: canonical JSON per document, organized BY MODEL VERSION
│   └── v2/policy_0001.json
├── results/comparisons/         # compare_models runs, downloaded from Blob (§6)
├── metrics/{version}/           # OUTPUT: per-doc metrics + summary.json
└── test_data/                   # git-ignored; read by no code: documents enter by import + OCR
```
`extraction_registry.json` (§5) is appended by `run_batch` (through `append_registry`) after each document's
results and metrics are written; a document that failed writes no entry.

### 2. `testing/prompts/*.prompt.txt`
One per ACORD form (25, 125, 140), one for Loss Runs, one for the bare `policy` (a package policy, an unclassified line) and one per policy line of business registered in the canonical family — each in `ocr_plus_image` and `image_only` — plus, for every common-model line, one per section group (`decl`, `arrays`, `lineblk`): the window prompts serving actually sends. Each contains the role framing, the **injected JSON schema for that selection pulled from the IMPL-01 registry** (so it stays in sync), the extraction rules (null-handling, date formats, repeating table rows), and the modality-mode instruction line. They are generated (`python -m testing.render_prompts --write`), never hand-edited.

**Why one file per type, form and line (arch §17):** each has a different schema, different field semantics (a Loss Run's "valuation date" vs. an ACORD's "policy effective date"), and different edge cases. Separate files let a reviewer read exactly what each is asked, and one prompt can be tuned without risk to the others.

**These are the same prompts serving uses.** The harness does not read the files: it renders through `common.prompts` inside `serving.pipeline.extract`, as serving does. The files must resolve to the identical rendered prompt — `test_prompt_files_match_the_renderer` asserts this equality in a test rather than maintaining it by discipline.

**Canonical field mapping needs no change here** (master §1.4): the semantic glosses are schema `description` fields, so they propagate automatically through the existing schema injection. The one requirement is negative — **the extraction path must never import `common.aliases`**. Mapping surface labels to canonical keys is the model's job; a runtime lookup would cap the system at a hand-written list. `test_no_runtime_aliases` (IMPL-14) enforces this.

### 3. `testing/run_extraction.py` — the CLI

```bash
# User decides which model to run — base, v1, v2, v3, ... — on imported, OCR'd documents
python -m testing.run_extraction --model v2   --tenant T --split test --corpus v1
python -m testing.run_extraction --model base --tenant T --split test --corpus v1   # zero-shot baseline

# The base model with a training checkpoint applied per request, not merged
python -m testing.run_extraction --model base \
       --adapter /workspace/staging/adapters/scope/personal_lines/v1/<run>/checkpoint-280 \
       --tenant T --split val --corpus v1

# Or through the operator command surface (IMPL-13), which wraps this:
python -m orchestration.run extract --model v2 --tenant T --source-ids policy_0001 policy_0002

# Image-only mode (no OCR text), to test the no-OCR pathway
python -m testing.run_extraction --model v2 --tenant T --source-ids policy_0001 --mode image_only

# Extract without scoring against the gold labels
python -m testing.run_extraction --model v2 --tenant T --split test --corpus v1 --no-score
```

- `--model` resolves via IMPL-02 `resolve_model_version` — **the user never has to know paths, just the tag.** Accepted values:

  | Value | Resolves to |
  |---|---|
  | `base` | Untuned `Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct` at the pinned revision, **no adapter** |
  | `base` + `--adapter <folder>` | The base with that LoRA applied on every request, **not merged** — a `checkpoint-N` folder or a run's adapter folder, local or a Blob prefix (copied once to `/workspace/adapters`). Refused with any other `--model`, without `adapter_config.json`, above the engine's `max_lora_rank`, or with `enable_lora: false` |
  | `v1`, `v2`, … | The merged model, plus a graduated per-type adapter where the routed type has one |
  | `v2` + `--format fp8` | That version's FP8 serving weights (refused while the version is still staged). A GGUF format here is a category error: llama.cpp loads those, and the endpoint runs vLLM |

  **`base` is not a curiosity** — it is the zero-shot baseline of the pilot protocol (IMPL-15) and the day-zero pre-annotation path (IMPL-04). Supporting it here means those two have one implementation, not three.
- **Which documents:** `--source-ids …` or `--split train|val|test` with `--corpus vN` (that corpus manifest's `source_ids_by_split`) — mutually exclusive; with neither, every labelled document of `--doc-type` (default `policy`) for `--tenant`. `--limit N` keeps the first N.
- `--mode ocr_plus_image` (default) | `image_only` — both production pathways testable from one harness.
- **Scoring is on by default**: a document with a gold label in `golden-labels/` is scored against it; one without, or whose gold has no review record (decision 2026-10-22), is extracted and not scored — the metrics file says why (`ground_truth: not scored: …`) and `summary.json` lists it under `not_scored`. `--no-score` extracts without comparing.
- Additional: `--label` (the name results are filed under; default the tag, or `base+<run>-<checkpoint>` with `--adapter`), `--format`, `--limit`, `--tenant`.

**What it does, step by step (arch §17):**
```
For each source_id:
  1. Read             → page count from processed/…/ocr_meta.json; every page image (cached in
                        ocr_cache/) and MinerU's per-page markdown as page_texts (none under
                        image_only); the label metadata (acord_form, lob) and the gold label
  2. Doc type         → known: --doc-type with the label's ACORD form (StaticClassifier);
                        the policy's line from the label metadata (classic auto → personal auto)
  3. Prompt select    → rendered by common.prompts for that type, form, line and window group
  4. Assemble input   → system = prompt (schema + instructions)
                        user   = [page image + page marker (+ OCR markdown, unless image_only)] per page
  5. Model inference  → the selected model generates JSON + token logprobs (policies: in windows over
                        every page, merged)
  6. Confidence       → per-field confidence from logprobs; no release calibrators are loaded, so the
                        identity transform applies and every field is flagged for review
  7. Validate         → serving's audit verdict (serving.pipeline.schema_verdict): JSON parses +
                        conforms to that selection's schema (strict_schema=False: an invalid
                        answer is kept and flagged schema:invalid)
  8. Write result     → results/{version}/{source_id}.json, with all_fields
  9. Metrics          → scored against the gold label → metrics/{version}/{source_id}.metrics.json
 10. Summarise        → metrics/{version}/summary.json; a failed document is listed, the batch carries on
```

**Steps 2–7 are executed by calling `serving/pipeline.py` (IMPL-11)** — the routine is a thin CLI wrapper over the serving pipeline plus document reading and metrics. It must **NOT re-implement inference**; doing so breaks test == prod, which is the entire point of the harness.

### 4. Metrics output

**Results `results/{version}/{doc}.json`** — the serving output contract (`ExtractionResult.as_dict()`: `extraction` is the answer — for a policy, the canonical JSON with every key of its line's schema — then `fields`, `list_fields`, `line_of_business`, `pages_used`, `review_flags`, `route`, and `reconciliation` for a Loss Run) with **`all_fields`** inserted after `list_fields`: every field of the selection's schema (`testing.comparison.field_report`) — those neither the answer nor the gold holds too — each with its `value` and `status` (`extracted`, `null`, `no_rows`, `not_in_output`, `system`, `structural`), a written value's `confidence` and `page_ref`, its review `flags`, and against a gold label its `gold` value and `result` (`correct`, `wrong`, `missed`, `invented`, `empty`); plus a `summary` of counts and the `document_flags`.

**Per-doc `metrics/{version}/{doc}.metrics.json`** (arch §17):
```json
{
  "document": "policy_0001",
  "model_version": "v2",
  "doc_type": "policy",
  "mode": "ocr_plus_image",
  "schema_valid": true,
  "overall_confidence": 0.88,
  "pages_used": [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9],
  "review_flags": ["coverages[3].limit:merge_conflict", "window_failed:arrays:p9"],
  "fields": {
    "policy.policy_number":   {"value": "...", "confidence": 0.94, "correct": true},
    "policy.expiration_date": {"value": "...", "confidence": 0.71, "correct": false}
  },
  "reference_accuracy": 0.91,
  "coverage_code_accuracy": 0.95,
  "field_exact_match_rate": 0.91,
  "field_normalized_match_rate": 0.93,
  "field_accuracy_by_field": {"...": "..."},
  "list_field_f1": {"coverages": 0.88},
  "list_field_recall": {"coverages": 0.83},
  "missed_rows": {"coverages": 2},
  "failures": [{"field": "policy.expiration_date", "expected": "...", "got": "..."}]
}
```
All correctness uses IMPL-08 metrics — `score_fields`, `score_all_list_fields`, and `values_agree` for each field's `correct` (dates, amounts and names normalised, numbers compared by their declared type) — the same definition of "correct" as the promotion gate. The gold label is narrowed to what the line's schema can hold (`common.canonical.schema_label`) and the system-filled fields are left out of both sides. On a **common-model line** both sides are narrowed, ids are compared through what their links name (`common.structural_ids.comparable_for`), table values are scored (`core_for_scoring`, as `run_eval.score_subset` does), and links, codes and overflow get numbers of their own (`CommonModelTally`: `reference_accuracy`, `coverage_code_accuracy`, `coverage_code_recall`, `additional_fields_recall`, `additional_fields_precision`). Without a gold label the file says `"ground_truth": "not supplied — confidence reported, accuracy not measurable"`.

**Batch summary `metrics/{version}/summary.json`** — aggregates across the batch for that model version: document count, schema-validity rate, mean confidence, mean latency per document, documents needing review, per-doc-type and per-mode counts, the failed documents with their errors, and — over the scored documents — mean field normalized match and mean list-field recall. **This is the file compared across model versions to answer "did v3 beat v2?"** — the testing-time mirror of the IMPL-08 promotion gate.

**On the confidence-without-ground-truth case (arch §17):** even with no golden JSON — a brand-new unknown document — the routine still emits `overall_confidence` and per-field confidence. That is the whole payoff of the calibration work: confidence is available at inference time on documents you have never labeled, which is exactly what routes low-confidence extractions to human review in production. (The harness loads no release calibrators, so its numbers are the identity transform of the raw signal, and every field is flagged; a release's calibrated confidence is measured by the golden eval, IMPL-08.)

### 5. `testing/extraction_registry.json`
One entry per extraction, so an output JSON is never ambiguous about its origin — written by `append_registry`, which `run_batch` calls after each document's results and metrics are written (§1):
```json
{
  "extractions": [
    {
      "document": "policy_0001",
      "doc_type": "policy",
      "model_version": "v2",
      "quant_format": "fp8",
      "mode": "ocr_plus_image",
      "result_path": "results/v2/policy_0001.json",
      "metrics_path": "metrics/v2/policy_0001.metrics.json",
      "overall_confidence": 0.88,
      "schema_valid": true,
      "review_flags": [],
      "extracted_at": "2026-02-15T11:03:00Z"
    }
  ]
}
```
Combined with the `results/{version}/` layout, anyone can see at a glance that `results/v2/policy_0001.json` came from model v2 — from the path *and* the registry. `model_version` here is the same tag that resolves to a `run_manifest.json` (IMPL-02), so an extracted result traces all the way back to the corpus version and git commit that produced the model that generated it.

### 6. `testing/compare_models.py` — base vs base + adapter
```bash
python -m testing.compare_models --input policy.pdf --gold policy_gold.json --lob homeowners \
    --reviewer-id <reviewer> --review-date YYYY-MM-DD --adapter <checkpoint-N>
python -m testing.compare_models --input data/bundles/<document>/ --adapter <adapter folder>
python -m testing.compare_models --input <folder of document folders> --adapter <adapter folder>
```
- `--input` is a PDF (with `--gold` and `--lob`), a bundle folder (`document.pdf` + `golden.json` + `metadata.json`), or a folder of them. Each document is imported and OCR'd (in `/workspace/venv-ocr`) under its own tenant — `compare` by default, never `default` or the real data's — and a re-run finds it by checksum. A gold is compared only with a review record (decision 2026-10-22): a PDF's `--gold` takes `--reviewer-id` and `--review-date`, written into its `metadata.json`; a bundle folder carries its own. Without one the answers are compared with no gold.
- One model load (`load_model("base", adapter=…)`, and `without_adapter` for the base on the same engine); each document is extracted twice through `serving.pipeline.extract`, by the base alone and with the adapter applied, not merged. No calibrators: every field is flagged in both.
- Per document under `--out` (default `testing/results/comparisons/<label>-<stamp>/`): `document.pdf`, `gold.json`, `base.json`, `adapter.json` (canonical JSON, every schema key), `base.all_fields.json`, `adapter.all_fields.json`, and `comparison.xlsx` (Summary and Fields sheets: each field's gold, base and adapter values, results, and `fixed`/`broke`). Table rows are matched by their identifier (a VIN, a form number, a coverage name), not by position. Across documents: `summary.xlsx` (one row per document, pooled totals) and `errors.json` when a route failed.
- The finished folder is uploaded to Blob under `exports/{tenant}/comparisons/{run}/` (the raw container: it holds policy data), checked file by file, and removed from the pod (`--keep-on-pod` keeps it). The command prints the `azcopy` line that downloads it to the laptop, by default into `D:\Fine-Tuning\testing\results\comparisons` (git-ignored; `--download-to` changes it).

## Constraints
- Confidence emitted even without ground truth.
- **Same prompts and same pipeline as serving — reuse, never reimplement.**
- Documents are read from the OCR the pipeline wrote (`processed/`), produced by the pinned MinerU (IMPL-03); the corpus build refuses documents read by more than one MinerU. The harness does not re-check the pin.
- No PII in logs; `results/`, `metrics/`, `ocr_cache/` and `test_data/` are gitignored, and comparison runs leave the pod through the raw container only.

## Acceptance checklist
- [ ] `run_extraction --model v2 --split test --corpus v1` writes `results/v2/*.json`.
- [ ] `--model base` runs the untuned base model with no adapter and writes `results/base/*.json`.
- [ ] `--model base --adapter <checkpoint>` applies the LoRA per request without merging, and files results under `base+<run>-<checkpoint>` (or `--label`); `--adapter` with any other `--model` is refused.
- [ ] `orchestration.run extract` produces byte-identical output to calling `run_extraction.py` directly.
- [ ] `--mode image_only` runs with no OCR and still extracts.
- [ ] Per-field + overall confidence present **with and without** ground truth.
- [ ] `line_of_business` present in every result, with confidence, `null` when undetermined.
- [ ] With a gold label that has a review record, per-doc + summary metrics computed, including list-field recall; `--no-score` skips them; a gold with no review record is not scored, and the metrics file and `summary.json` (`not_scored`) say why.
- [ ] Every result carries `all_fields`: every field of its schema, with its value and, against gold, its result.
- [ ] `--format fp8` runs that version's FP8 serving weights once packaged (a GGUF format is a category error here: the harness runs vLLM).
- [ ] `extraction_registry.json` links result → model version → run manifest.
- [ ] `testing/prompts/*.prompt.txt` render identically to `common.prompts` for the same inputs (asserted, not assumed).
- [ ] **Output equals the serving pipeline's output on the same input** (parity test).
- [ ] `compare_models` writes base and adapter JSON, both `all_fields` reports and `comparison.xlsx` per document, `summary.xlsx` across them, under its own tenant, and uploads the run to `exports/{tenant}/comparisons/`.

---

## Current implementation (2026-10-21)

- Both entries run the extraction: `python -m testing.run_extraction …` and
  `python -m orchestration.run extract …`, which passes the same arguments to `run_extraction.main`. Extraction
  goes through `serving.pipeline.extract` — windows and the window merge for policies, the serving guards,
  every-key output, dates in `MM/DD/YYYY` — so test output is production output.
- **Every page is read** (decision 2026-10-19, IMPL-11 §4): a policy of any length is read in windows over
  all its pages, never on selected pages, so `pages_used` lists every page and a long policy takes more
  windows than it did. Results written before 2026-10-21 read a policy longer than five pages on its selected
  pages only. Nothing in the harness selects pages: there is no page threshold and no `long_doc_types`.
- Documents are read from Blob as the pipeline wrote them (`document_request`): `processed/` page images and
  MinerU markdown, `golden-labels/` gold and metadata. A document not yet OCR'd is refused with what to run;
  one failing document is recorded in `summary.json` and the batch carries on (exit 1 only when every document
  failed).
- A policy's line comes from its label metadata (`lob`); without it the fallback canonical schema (the common
  model) is used. No serving plan is passed, so nothing is refused for its line.
- No calibrators are loaded: confidence is the identity transform and every field is flagged for review. This
  run measures extraction, not a release's review thresholds.
- `schema_valid` and the `schema:invalid` flag are serving's own verdict (`serving.pipeline.schema_verdict`, IMPL-11
  §5): on a common-model line the served answer, every key filled, is what is judged.
- The gold is read from `golden-labels/` under the form-edition rule where it holds — a common-model policy
  line (`document_request` → `label_rules.under_edition_rule`) — as the gate's golden eval reads the frozen set
  (IMPL-08): the served answer there has a form's edition moved out of its number into `edition_date` (IMPL-11),
  and a gold imported before that rule is not rewritten in Blob, so scored as stored it would count the
  convention wrong. A self-contained line's gold is scored as stored: nothing on it moves an edition.
- A gold with no review record (`export_golden_labels.review_problem`, decision 2026-10-22; a twin inherits its
  seed's) is not scored: `document_request` returns no gold and logs why, the extraction still runs and is
  written, the metrics file records `ground_truth: not scored: …`, and `summary.json` lists it under
  `not_scored`. `compare_models` reads golds the same way.
- On the pod `run_extraction` and `compare_models` run detached in tmux like every long job; they survive a
  closed laptop.
- The gate's numbers do not come from this harness: they come from the frozen golden eval set
  (IMPL-08 `golden_eval.evaluate_version`), through the same serving pipeline.

## Changes since 2026-10-05

- Added the alignment line and dated *Current implementation* 2026-10-13.
- Goal and §1: documents are imported and OCR'd ones read from Blob, not PDFs dropped into `test_data/` (now unread); added `compare_models.py`, `comparison.py`, `render_prompts.py`, `results/comparisons/`, `summary.json`; the registry file is not written by the CLI.
- §2: real prompt file names (per ACORD form, per line, image-only variants, window prompts per group); the harness renders through `common.prompts` rather than reading the files.
- §3: replaced `--input`/`--ground-truth` with `--source-ids` / `--split` + `--corpus` / `--tenant` / `--doc-type` / `--no-score` / `--label`; added `--model base --adapter <checkpoint>`; corrected `--format` (FP8 serving weights, not GGUF); rewrote the step list (read from Blob, static classification, identity calibration, `strict_schema=False`, all_fields, summary).
- §4: results JSON carries `all_fields`; per-doc metrics keys as written (typed `values_agree`, common-model scoring and tally metrics); summary is `summary.json` with the aggregates actually computed (no ECE or per-value LoB accuracy).
- §5: noted that `append_registry` is not called by `run_batch`.
- §6 (new): `compare_models` — inputs, compare tenant, outputs (`base/adapter.json`, `*.all_fields.json`, `comparison.xlsx`, `summary.xlsx`), upload to `exports/{tenant}/comparisons/{run}/` and the azcopy download.
- Constraints and acceptance checklist updated to match.
- `run_batch` now writes `extraction_registry.json` (commit f5668a4).

## Changes since 2026-10-13

- Header: aligned with the code of 2026-10-21; architecture reference `finetuning-architecture-v2.4.docx`.
- §3: a policy is read in windows over every page; validation is serving's audit verdict (`schema_verdict`).
- §4: the `pages_used` example lists every page.
- §5: `run_batch` writes the registry (the stale "does not call today" removed).
- *Current implementation*: every page read, no page selection, results before 2026-10-21 read selected pages; `schema_valid` is serving's verdict; golds read under the form-edition rule, as the golden eval reads them.

## Changes of 2026-10-22 (code review)

- §2 and *Current implementation*: a gold with no review record (decision 2026-10-22) is extracted and not scored, and the metrics file and `summary.json` (`not_scored`) say why.
- *Current implementation*: golds are read under the form-edition rule only on common-model policy lines (`label_rules.under_edition_rule`).
- §6: `compare_models --reviewer-id --review-date` give a PDF's `--gold` its review record; without one the answers are compared with no gold.
- Acceptance checklist: scoring needs a reviewed gold.
